The Accessibility extension for Visual Studio Code is not an official or maintained extension. However, you can find the necessary information about it in the [VS Code Marketplace](https://marketplace.visualstudio.com/items?itemName=visualstudiocode.vscode-accessibility).

Here are the steps to get the `package.json` and code for the Accessibility extension:

1. Open Visual Studio Code.
2. Go to the Extensions view by clicking on the square icon in the left sidebar or pressing `Ctrl+Shift+X`.
3. Search for "Accessibility" in the search bar.
4. Click on the "Accessibility" extension from the search results.
5. In the top-right corner of the extension card, click on the "Download" button to download and install the extension.

Once the extension is installed, you can find the `package.json` file by navigating to the `.vscode/extensions` directory in your workspace root folder. The `package.json` file contains metadata about the extension, such as its name, version, description, authors, and dependencies.

Here is an example of what a `package.json` file might look like for the Accessibility extension:

```json
{
  "name": "vscode-accessibility",
  "version": "0.2.0",
  "description": "Accessibility tools for Visual Studio Code.",
  "author": {
    "name": "Microsoft Corporation",
    "url": "https://www.microsoft.com"
  },
  "license": "MIT",
  "engines": {
    "vscode": "^1.35.0"
  },
  "scripts": {
    "start": "vsce debug start",
    "debug": "vsce debug launch"
  },
  "dependencies": {},
  "devDependencies": {}
}
```

In this example, the `name` field is `"vscode-accessibility"`, the `version` field is `"0.2.0"`, and the `description` field is a brief description of the extension. The `author` field contains information about the extension's author, including their name and URL. The `license` field specifies the license under which the extension is distributed.

The `engines` field lists the minimum version of Visual Studio Code that the extension requires. The `scripts` field defines commands that can be used to start and debug the extension. The `dependencies` and `devDependencies` fields are empty in this example, but you may need to add additional dependencies or dev dependencies depending on your requirements.

I hope this helps! Let me know if you have any further questions.
